2026年10月30日
全球宏观 
预期之内的暂停——10月美联储会议点评
预期之内的暂停加息，但加息周期还未结束。10月议息会议上，美联储暂停
加息，政策利率的目标维持在3.75%-4%，符合市场预期。会议前，市场对10
月不加息的定价超过为九成，不加息被期货市场认为是基准情形。我们认为，
10月会议正好在美国中期选举之前，美联储加息的动力不足。此次10月会议
中，有两位委员投了反对票，认为10月就应该加息。12月再次加息概率超过
八成，仍是基准情形。
9月通胀数据略超预期，但还没到不得不加息的地步。9月美国CPI环比上涨
0.6%，较8月的0.4%进一步加速，同比由3.4%升至3.7%，高于市场预期的
3.6%；核心CPI环比上涨0.3%，与8月持平，高于预期的0.2%，同比由8月
的2.4%升至2.6%。总体通胀回升主要由能源推动，油价维持高位，而核心通
胀环比增速仍高，通胀回落进程面临新的阻力。同时，美国9月新增非农仅
2.9万，低于市场预期，也给了美联储等待更多数据的理由。
美国经济衰退风险仍低，美联储仍有加息空间。10月会议美联储不公布新的
经济预期概要，因而无法从委员预测中获得更新。数据上看，美国经济尚未
出现明显走弱，此前6-7月世界杯或带来了额外的季节性干扰。9月非季调的
新增非农仍略强于去年。非季调的非农9月增加33.3万人，去年9月增加
30.5万人。经季调后新增非农的2.9万人则低于去年同期修正后的7.6万人。
芝加哥联储的金融条件指数的非金融杠杆分项在衰退前往往会明显上行，但
目前来看也仍处于低位。这为年内美联储加息提供了一定空间。
美联储将从2027年开始调整议息会议安排。美联储主席凯文·沃什在记者会上
透露，虽然每年八次会议对联邦公开市场委员会（FOMC）来说是“合适的数
量”，但从2027年开始，将有一次会议专注于宏观货币政策战略，不进行利
率投票，也不发布政策声明；2028年此类战略会议将增至两次，政策设定会
议相应减少至六次。
流动性指标显示整体流动性仍处于充裕区间。担保隔夜融资利率99分位和1
分位的利差在2019年9月曾出现了显著的扩大，近期的数据上看，这个利差
大概在十几基点波动。美联储负债占名义GDP的比重下降到了约20.5%，距离
2019年的低点17.1%的差距约为3.4%。芝加哥联储金融条件指数也仍处于宽
松的负值区间，不过杠杆分项指数略有上行。
短端利率下行，美股上涨。会议当日收盘，美股方面三大股指都上涨，其中
标普500上涨0.28%、纳指上涨0.27%、道指上涨0.19%，标普行业指数里11
个行业中仅有3个下跌，分别是公用事业、房地产和必需消费，而信息技术、
能源和金融的涨幅靠前。会议后美债短端收益率普遍下行，3个月下降4个基
点，2年下降3个基点，美元小幅走弱，黄金下跌约0.56%。短期美国股市或
受益于暂停加息，但加息周期仍未结束，未来或仍有压力。
王博群, PhD, FRM
hugh.wang@bocomgroup.com
(852) 3766 1853

### Page 8

评级定义
分析员个股评级定义 : 分析员行业评级定义：
买入：预期个股未来12个月的总回报高于相关行业。
中性：预期个股未来12个月的总回报与相关行业一致。
沽出：预期个股未来12个月的总回报低于相关行业
无评级：对于个股未来12个月的总回报与相关行业的比较，
分析员并无确信观点。
领先：分析员预期所覆盖行业未来12个月的表现相对于大盘
标杆指数具吸引力。
同步：分析员预期所覆盖行业未来12个月的表现与大盘标杆
指数一致。
落后：分析员预期所覆盖行业未来12个月的表现相对于大盘
标杆指数不具吸引力。
香港市场的标杆指数为恒生综合指数，A股市场的标杆指数
为MSCI中国A股指数，美国上市中概股的标杆指数为标普美
国中概股50（美元）指数

等公司的高级人员或其同级人员）；iii）他们及他们之相关有联系者并没拥有于本报告内涉及其评论的任何公司的证券之任何财务利益。
根据证监会持牌人或注册人操守准则第16.2段，“有联系者”指：i）分析员的配偶、亲生或领养的未成年子女，或未成年继子女；ii）某
信托的受托人，而分析员、其配偶、其亲生或领养的未成年子女或其未成年继子女是该信托的受益人或酌情对象；或iii）惯于或有义务按
照分析员的指示或指令行事的另一人。

日本：7月以降の設備投資需要はAI関連産業を中心に旺盛
3
 7～9月期の機械受注は増勢回復へ。AI関連業種からの受注がけん引
― ①旺盛なAI需要のもと、関連業種では設備不足感が強まっていたこと、②輸出増が持続するもとで、好業績が期待され
ること、③中東情勢の不確実性が後退したことなどが背景
 先行き設備投資は緩やかな増勢を辿る見込み
― 半導体製造装置をはじめ、AI・データセンター関連品目を中心に受注残が増加しており、目先こうした傾向が続く見通し
― 積み上がった受注残を段階的に消化することで、設備投資が持続的に下支えされる公算が大きい

世界：AI関連投資の拡大が鉱物需要を押し上げる一方、鈍い資源開発投資の回復ペース
4
 AI関連需要の拡大が銅やレアアースの需給環境に影響
― データセンター建設や電力インフラ整備に伴い、銅やレアアースの需要が拡大。ガリウム等の戦略鉱物への需要も増加
 資源開発投資の回復が遅れるなか、中長期的な供給不足への懸念強まる
―IEAは、2035年に銅が供給量対比で約26％の供給不足に陥ると予測。レアアースは供給不足を回避する見込みながら、

採掘・精錬において中国が支配的地位を占めており、供給元の集中がリスク

世界：AI投資が金融市場を下支えする一方、持続性への懸念は残存
 IMFはAI投資や企業収益の拡大を背景に金融市場が底堅さを維持していると評価
― ただし、金融安定リスクは引き続き高いとの評価で、AI、国債市場、サイバーを主要なリスク要因として指摘
 AIについてはAI主導の市場調整リスクを警戒
―AI中核企業の収益拡大によって株価の割高感は緩和する一方、社債発行や循環的な資金調達が拡大。投資収益が期
待を下回った場合、金融市場への影響が増幅される可能性

 主要国の長期金利は、期待短期金利とタームプレミアムの双方から上昇

世界：ヘッジファンドのレバレッジ拡大が市場ショックを増幅、銀行部門への波及にも警戒
 高まるヘッジファンドの存在感と市場への影響力
― ヘッジファンドの総資産は2013年の約4兆ドルから2026年初には約13兆ドルに拡大。平時には市場の流動性や効率性
を支える一方、ストレス時にはレバレッジ解消や取引集中が価格変動を増幅させる懸念
 ヘッジファンドの損失がプライムブローカーを通じて銀行部門に波及するリスク
―IMFはヘッジファンドの運用成績悪化がプライムブローカーの収益にも悪影響を及ぼすと分析。銀行自身の健全性に加
え、ノンバンクとの相互連関を通じた金融システム全体への波及にも警鐘

11
 EUと中国は、貿易不均衡の是正措置について暫定合意。中国からのハイブリッド車輸入は従来見通しの半分以下に
―EUが中国産EVに追加関税を賦課した2024年以降、追加関税の対象とならないハイブリッド車輸入が急増
― 欧州委員会の試算では、今回の措置によって2030年までの4年間で数百万台の輸入を削減見込み。直近では、EUが輸
入するハイブリッド車の約半分を中国車が占めており、域内自動車市場に一定のインパクト

 ASEANの消費は中東情勢緊迫化後に減速も、概して底堅く推移
― インドネシア・タイは4～6月期に大きめに落ち込んだものの足元で持ち直しの動き。フィリピン、マレーシアは減速が続く
も大崩れを回避、ベトナムは堅調さを維持
 背景に各国個別要因や消費マインド悪化の歯止め
― タイは消費刺激策（6～11月）、マレーシアはインフレの抑制、ベトナムは高い賃金上昇率が消費を下支え
― 消費マインドは中東情勢緊迫化後の悪化に歯止め。もっとも、中東危機の長期化が重しに
アジア：ASEANの消費は足元で総じて底堅い動き
17
（注） タイは個人消費指数。フィリピンはGDPの実質個人消費、その他は実質小売指数。
ベトナム、マレーシアは総合CPIを用いたみずほ総合研究所による実質化。四半
期ベース。マレーシアとタイの直近値は7・8月の前年同期比
（出所） 各国統計、CEICより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

日本：コメ価格下落で企業物価は鈍化。もっとも、先行きは原油高を背景に再加速へ
18
 9月の国内企業物価はコメ価格の下落を主因に、前年比＋6.8％（前月：同＋7.6％）と鈍化
― 新米価格が昨年を下回ったこと等を背景に、コメ価格が大幅に下落。国内企業物価を▲0.75%Pt押し下げ
― 一方、3月来の原油高を背景に化学・プラスチック製品は高止まり。また、AI需要等を背景に非鉄金属も高い伸びを維持
 7月以降の原油高再燃を背景に、先行きの国内企業物価は再加速する見込み
― 原油価格高止まりにタンカー運賃の急騰が重なり、先行きの輸入物価は原油関連を中心に加速へ
― 国内企業物価は石油・石炭製品やラグを伴いつつ化学・プラスチック製品などを中心に再加速する見込み
（注） コメは玄米及び精米。食品関連は、飲食料品及び農林水産物（除くコメ）

（出所） 日本銀行「企業物価指数」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

電子產業組 王穎
2026年10月27日
記憶體需求不止
記憶體產業專題

### Page 2

1
簡報大綱
簡報大綱
AI需求延續打破循環
推論需求擴大，帶動儲存市場規模提升
推薦個股

### Page 3

2
結論
結論
記憶體產業歷經庫存調整與價格下修後，走出谷底進入上升週期。
AI 應用快速擴展下HBM、DDR5產能持續緊俏，供不應求走勢延續。
全球伺服器市場及AI推論需求擴大，帶動儲存市場規模擴大
推薦個股：南亞科(2408)、華邦電(2344)、群聯(8299)、宜鼎(5289)。

### Page 4

3
受AI爆炸性成長打破循環
DRAM市場過往約每3～4年形成一個完整供需週期。隨AI需求爆炸性成長，三大原廠相繼EOL舊
世代產品轉向全力生產高階產品導致排擠 ，因此導致價格直線式飆升，當價格上漲至一定程度時，
消費性產品轉嫁能力有限導致需求減少 ，然而AI需求仍維持強勁導致供給無法滿足需求 ，預期至
2027年仍維持供不應求態勢。
資料來源：Trendforce，兆豐投顧整理
DRAM 供需比
AI需求延續打破循環

### Page 5

4
預期DRAM價格維持高檔
DRAM 市場在 2025 年下半年迎來顯著的價格攀升，2026年全年DDR4、DDR5價格持續走高，
AI持續爆炸性成長之下，持續搶佔記憶體顆粒產品，雖消費性產品已開始面臨價格轉嫁困境導致
拉貨力道減弱，但記憶體顆粒廠商仍無法滿足 AI需求，因此預期記憶體價格將維持高檔 ，然LTA
比例提高預期漲價幅度將逐步放緩。
資料來源：Trendforce，兆豐投顧彙整
DRAM價格走勢
AI需求延續打破循環

受記憶體價格上漲衝擊，PC 與手機等消費性終端產品需求受到壓抑，2026 年至 2027 年出貨
量預估持續呈現負成長，然而伺服器強勁出貨動能帶動下，2026、2027年出貨量年增率分別達

達 13% 與 17%，伺服器佔整體記憶體用量比重持續攀升逾40%，龐大的運算需求有效填補並推
升用量缺口，促使整體DRAM市場維持供不應求的緊俏態勢。

### Page 9

8
記憶體製程持續推進
資料來源： 兆豐投顧整理
記憶體廠商持續推進微縮製程，核心目的在於縮小晶片面積以增加每片晶圓的晶片產出量、顯
著降低每單位容量的生產成本，並同時實現更高儲存容量、更快傳輸速度與更低功耗，以搶佔
AI 與新世代伺服器等高階市場的競爭優勢。
DRAM Process Node Roadmap
AI需求延續打破循環

### Page 10

9
打破記憶體牆（Memory Wall）
資料來源：KAIST TERALAB、兆豐投顧整理
AI 大型語言模型（LLM）參數持續擴大下，GPU 運算核心的算力成長遠快於記憶體存取速度，
單純靠提高傳輸頻率來擴展頻寬已面臨物理瓶頸，因此，HBM4 選擇將 I/O 匯流排寬度從行之
已久的 1,024-bit 翻倍至 2,048-bit，將顯著提升算力利用率。並且HBM3E消耗3倍的DDR5晶圓。
HBM4消耗3.5倍，HBM4E會消耗5倍的DDR5晶圓，HBM持續迭代下將會消耗更多記憶體晶圓。
HBM製程進度
AI需求延續打破循環

### Page 11

10
DRAM原廠擴產有限
資料來源： Trendforce、兆豐投顧整理
原廠於2027年擴產力道有限，而AI Server 搭載的 HBM結構複雜，生產過程將大量消耗先進製
程產能，同時NVIDIA新型伺服器搭載大量LPDDR5，預計將占盡高階產能將近 70%。在總體
產能擴張受限且 HBM 消耗量極大的雙重壓力下，傳統 DRAM 的供給空間將被進一步擠壓，這
將支撐高階記憶體市場的價格韌性，並促使產能轉向更高毛利的 AI 相關應用。

### Page 12

11
AI Server消耗大量DRAM產能
資料來源： Trendforce、兆豐投顧整理
2027 年AI 晶片對 HBM的需求量不斷攀升，進而大量消耗記憶體原廠產能。由於單顆 GPU 搭
載的 HBM 容量不斷攀升，加上 HBM 的層數增加與生產良率限制，使得每生產一組 AI 系統就
需要投入極高比例的晶圓產能，尤其RUBIN及TPU系列之伺服器需求龐大。
原預估AI Server所消耗原廠DRAM高階產能達近70%，然產能不足導致NVIDIA採取降規，預估
達高階產能約50%。

全球 NAND 市場高度集中於前六大原廠，呈現典型的寡佔格局，而龍頭三星（29.3%）與 SK
海力士（18.2%）則因優先將產能調配至高利潤的 HBM 生產，致使其 NAND 市佔率面臨下滑
趨勢。

NAND FLASH市場呈現明顯的週期性循環，每約3.5～4年進入一個完整供需週期，過往NAND
FLASH市場都相較穩定，然而AI快速發展下帶動儲存供需缺口擴大，預期2027年HDD需求持續
強勁且eSSD滲透率提升下，將持續供不應求態勢。

### Page 21

NAND製程發展趨勢
資料來源：兆豐投顧整理
目前主流製程為 176 層～238 層，其中 Samsung V8、Micron G8、Kioxia BiCS8、SK hynix
V8 是企業級 SSD 常見使用製程。
雖2025~2026 年起將正式跨入 300 層以上世代（3xxL、V9/V10），儲存密度、效能與功耗管
理將同步優化，V8進入V9世代可透過舊設備更新生產 ，然而進入V10世代則須購買新機台 ，對
於NAND原廠資本支出較有壓力因此普及仍需時間。

### Page 1

M
Idea
Chroma Ate Inc.  | Asia Pacific
2H26 Driven by Semis and
Photonics; 2027 Seen as
Another Strong Year
A unique combination of power, electrical and optical testing
tool capabilities position the company well in AI deployment.
Stay OW.
We see 3Q26 EPS of NT$13.71: Chroma's 3Q26 revenue came in at NT$17,926mn,
up 32% QoQ and 180% YoY. We believe the strength was driven mainly by the
semiconductor and photonics businesses, while the power testing business likely

remained at an elevated level. Within the semiconductor and photonics businesses,
SL T  should benefit from AI HPC customers' ramping; meanwhile, optical testing
tools for transceivers and, to a lesser extent, CPO, are also seen as growth drivers
during the quarter. We estimate GM of 60.5% (flat QoQ) as scale tailwinds should
be largely offset by a less favorable product mix, while OPM should expand by
0.5ppt QoQ to 39.3% on some operating leverage. Overall, we expect EPS of NT

$13.71 (+13% QoQ/+15% YoY).
Growth momentum seen sustained into 2027: Looking ahead, we expect Chroma
to see another year of strong growth, supported by the power testing (51% growth
for 42% mix) and semi & photonics businesses (58% growth for 40% mix).
• Power testing: The ever-increasing power consumption of AI clusters should
continue to drive demand for higher spec testing tools. Along with our
forecast of a 75% CAGR for global power supply unit demand in the next

three years (see report), we think this should stay a major driver for Chroma.
• SLT: We are seeing more HPC chip designers adopting the system level
testing process to make sure defects won't be incorporated into the AI
systems. As the first mover in this market. Chroma should benefit from
increasing chip volume as well as higher TDP, in our view.
• Photonics: Transceivers should continue to be the mainstream solution;
architectures such as CPO and NPO also need testing tools.

• Emerging business: We expect Chroma, by leveraging its existing customer
relationship and technology expertise, to gain more traction in FT handlers
and burn-in ovens for AI HPCs in 2027. These are the markets that Chroma
previously focused less on, so they represent new opportunities.
Stay OW: We remain constructive on Chroma for its testing equipment's unique
combination of power, electrical and optical capabilities, allowing it to capture
growth opportunities in various aspects of AI deployment. Our PT remains NT

$2,800 as our estimate increases are offset by a lower target multiple of 40x 2027e
P/E (previously 45x) to factor in generally more conservative sentiment toward the
AI space now. This implies a PEG of 0.6 (vs. 1.0 over the past decade).

Multiple drivers in the semiconductor business
System-level testing  (SL T) business
SL T should benefit from the capex cycle of NVIDIA's Rubin generation GPU, AMD's MI450
GPU and more ASIC accelerators.  Management pointed out that discrete testing tools are
still preferred  by major customers.  We are also seeing more HPC chip designers adopting

the system level testing process to make sure defects won't be incorporated into the AI
systems. We believe Chroma, as the first mover in this market, should benefit from
increasing chip volume, as well as higher TDP.
Metrology business
The metrology business should continue to benefit from current advanced packaging
architectures from its foundry and OSAT customers. It is confident that the performance
of its tools can win future architecture projects, which our industry checks suggest should

have higher metrology density.

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
Exhibit 3: Chroma –  Offerings for Metrology in Advanced Packaging
Source: Chroma
Chroma's metrology equipment employs white light interferometry technology. The basic
working principle is that white light is emitted on the reference mirror and the sample,
which are placed at the same distance from the light source. The reflected beams from
the reference mirror and sample surface are then received by a light sensor.
Interference occurs when two light beams collide, which could cause  them to either

strengthen or weaken, depending on the phase of the two light beams. Thus, by
measuring the intensity of the combined light beams, the distance between light sensor
and multiple points on the target surface can be calculated through an algorithm. This can
then be used to measure the dimensions of the structure or the alignment of layers.
Exhibit 4: Concept of Light Interference
Source: Keyence
Exhibit 5: Setup of Interferometer
Source: Wikipedia
CPO contribution to start from 2026

Chroma has been qualified for optical engine testing in Insertion 3 and system-level
testing in Insertion 4, and there might be incremental opportunities if supply chain players'
progress falls behind for testing equipment in Insertion 2. Over the longer term, Chroma
also  aspires to participate in the PIC optical/electrical/reliability testing in Insertion 1.

believe this is a natural extension of its expertise and customer relationships accumulated
in SL Ts. We expect Chroma to gain more traction in FT handlers for AI HPCs in 2027.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Our price target (base case scenario value) remains  NT$2,800: Our PT is based on a
target multiple of 40x 2027e P/E (vs. 45x 2027e previously).   We lowered our multiple to
factor in recent volatility in market sentiment toward AI.   We view our multiple as justified
based on our estimated 68% operating profit CAGR over 2025-28. This implies a PEG of
0.6 vs. its average of 1.0 in the past decade, when the stock was trading at an average of

~19x forward P/E with an operating profit CAGR of 20%.
Bull case scenario value – NT$3,900 (unchanged): We apply a 55x multiple (vs. 62x P/E
previously)  to our earnings estimate for 2027.  This scenario reflects stronger  5G and HPC
demand, driving more earnings upside.
Bear case scenario value – NT$1,100 (unchanged): We apply a 16x P/E multiple (vs. 18x
P/E previously) to our earnings estimate for 2027. This scenario factors in weaker HPC
revenue as hyperscalers start to focus more on ROI for AI capex than they did in the past

couple of years.
Risks:
• Softer-than-expected AI capex
• Slower progress in advanced packaging capacity expansion
• Weaker consumer electronics and automobile related demand

Base case, 40x 2027e P/E. We use P/E as our valuation methodology because we think it
better captures Chroma's earnings growth momentum. The stock traded at an average
valuation of ~19x P/E during 2014-25 with an operating proﬁt CAGR of 20%, implying a PEG
of 1.0. We think our target multiple of 40x is justiﬁed by the 68% operating proﬁt CAGR we
project for 2025-28.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

M
 Update
ASE Technology Holding Co. Ltd.  | Asia Pacific
  CPO Revenue on Track; Stay 
OW 
 CPO is on track for >US$500mn in 2027: Management mentioned the CPO has 
already started mass production  and will reach at least US$500mn in 2027. This is 
in-line with our estimates that we forecast CPO revenue to be >US$600mn in 2027.
Slightly upward revision of LEAP revenue: The company slightly raised 2026 LEAP 
(leading-edge advanced packaging) revenue from US$3.7bn to US$4bn and  2027 
from >US$7.5bn to >US$8bn, on track  to our forecast of US$9bn. It expects at least 
US$1bn of full process CoWoS-like revenue in 2027 vs. MSe of US$1.8bn.
Margin expansion is also on track: 3Q26 ATM gross margin has already surpassed 
the structural range and reached 30.6%. The company mentioned 30% would be 
the new floor and expects it to continue to expand sequentially in 2027.
3Q26 GM and 4Q26 guidance slightly beat: 3Q26 GM of 22.1% is slightly higher 
than both MSe and the consensus. 4Q26 revenue guidance of 6-8% QoQ growth 
and GM of 23-24% are both slightly higher than MSe of 5% QoQ growth and 22-23% 
GM forecast.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
ASE Technology Holding Co. Ltd. (3711.TW)

growth rate of 10%, terminal growth rate of 3.5%, and cash dividend payout ratio of 60%.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger-than-expected global economic and semiconductor growth
n Margin expansion through merger consolidation
n New wearables project wins with higher margins
Risks to Downside

n Slower-than-expected global economic and semiconductor growth
n Further margin erosion amid price competition

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

2026.10.29   
HD현대중공업  (329180)

3Q26: 조선사는 우직해야 한다  Company Brief

3분기 매출 5조 7,536억원/영업이익 8,992억원 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 매출은 5조 7,536억원/영업이익은 8,992억원으로 
컨센서스(6조 1,295억원/9,671억원) 및 당사 추정치(6조 859억원/9,679억원) 대비 
매출 및 영업이익 각각 -6.1%/-7.0%, -5.5%/-7.1%를 하회하는 실적을 기록하였다.  
조선사업부 매출이 4조 3,988억원/영업이익 6,463억원(OPM 14.7%)이며 이중 상선 
OPM은 14.7%로, 2분기 17.8%에 비해 3.1%P 하락하였으나 1분기 15.9%와 크게 
차이나지 않는 수준을 유지하면서 조업일수 및 환율 하락에도 불구하고 P-MIX및 
빈티지 개선에 따른 효과를 보여주었다. 해양사업부는 매출 3,799억원/영업이익 
1,265억원(OPM 33.3%)로 지난 분기(15.9%)보다도 훨씬 높은 영업이익을 
기록하였다. 엔진기계사업부는 매출 9,511억원/영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)를 
기록하며 역시 견조한 실적을 기록하였다.  
일회성 이익으로 Trion 공사의 C/O 180 억원, 일회성 손실로는 셧다운에 따른 
조업감소 영향 500억원 및 환율하락에 따른 이익 감소분 450억원이 있었으며 금액은 
공개되지 않았으나 임단협 잠정타결에 따른 성과급/타결금 지급에 대한 추가 충당금의 
75% 인식이 반영되었다. 알려진 일회성만 제거하더라도 실질 영업이익률은 
17%이상으로 2분기 16.4%보다 높아진 호실적이라고 할 수 있다. 특히 RUYA 공사가 
본 궤도에 오르면서 해양사업부의 이익률이 괄목할 만큼 높아졌으며 지속가능하다는 
회사의 설명이다.

투자의견 BUY 및 조선업종 TOP PICK 유지, 목표주가 530,000원 하향 
HD현대중공업의 목표주가를 530,000원으로 38.3% 하향하지만, 투자의견 BUY와 
조선업종 TOP PICK 을 유지한다. 환율 하락 및 28년 하반기 엔진증설 매출확대, 
해양공사 이익률 상승 등 요인을 종합 반영하여 실적 추정치를 미세 반영하여 도출한 
2027F BPS 131,910원에 Target P/B를 기존대비 하향한 4.0배로 적용하였다. 특수선 
기대감 및 선가상승 기대감 하락에 따른 조선업종 전반의 밸류에이션 하락을 반영하여 
Target을 낮췄지만 그럼에도 조선업종 중 가장 높은 멀티플을 부여하며 Top 
Pick의견을 견지한다. 여러 악재에도 불구하고 HD현대중공업은 탄탄하다. 본업인 
상선사업부의 9월말 기준 수주량은 137.9억 달러로 목표치를 이미 초과 달성하였으며 
전사 기준으로도 수주목표 90.5%를 채워넣었다. 수주잔고 또한 상선 421억 
달러/전사 609억 달러로 각각 작년 말 대비 12.6%/13.2% 증가하였다. 해양부문과 
엔진기계부문은 조선사업부보다도 높은 이익률로 든든한 실적의 기반이 되고 있다.  
최근 HD현대그룹은 미국의 EPC강자 Kiewit과 미국 합작법인 설립에 관한 계약을 
맺고 그린필드 기반의 미국 신규 조선소 건설을 추진하고 있다. 빠르면 올해 중으로 
건립과 관련한 세부 사항이 공개될 예정이며 HD현대중공업이 중추적인 역할을 담당할 
것으로 보인다. 일찍부터 브라운필드 방식으로 접근한 동종사와 달리 다소 시간은 
걸렸지만 과감하게 결정된 투자다. 조선소 예상 준공 역시 2028년 이후로 예상되어 
당장 기업가치에 반영하기는 어려우나 엔진 증설과 함께 2028년 후에는 분명한 실적 
상향 요인이 될 것으로 예상한다. 우직하게 본업에 충실하며 신중하게 미래를 준비하는 
조선소, 귀감이다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상

 Overweight(비중확대) 
 Neutral(중립) 
 Underweight(비중축소)

Document 228: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

HD현대중공업의 3Q26 연결 실적은 매출액 5조 8,602억원(yoy +33%, qoq -7%), 
영업이익 9,161억원(yoy +64%, qoq -12%, OPM 15.6%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 하회할 
전망이다. 동사의 3Q26 조업일수는 여름휴가, 추석연휴 및 중대재해 등 영향으로 
qoq -21% 감소했으며 환율 역시 qoq -84원 감소한 영향을 감안해 전분기 대비 
실적을 보수적으로 추정했다. 그럼에도 해양 부문 Trion FPU발 C/O에 따른 일회성 
환입이 예상됨에 따라 일부 이익 상쇄가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 한편, 동사의 
임단협 부결로 당분기 추가적인 인건비 부담은 제한적일 것으로 판단되며 잠수함 
화재 사고 관련 비용 역시 4Q 이후 반영될 전망이다.

동사는 급격히 커져가는 AIDC향 전력수요 및 발전용 엔진 수주에 대응하고자 9/10 
총 4GW 규모의 4행정 중속엔진 캐파 증설을 위한 신규 투자를 공시헸다. 동사의 
기존 DC향 4행정 중속엔진 수주 단가를 고려 시 GW당 약 1조원의 매출 창출이 
가능할 전망이며 2행정 및 선박용 4행정 엔진 대비 높은 수익성이 예상되는 바 
생산이 본격화될 28년부터 동사 실적 성장성의 핵심 동인으로 작용할 전망이다.

동사에 대해 투자의견 BUY를 유지하며 업종 밸류에이션 디레이팅 및 시장 프리미엄 
조정에 따른 Target Multipe 변동으로 목표주가를 70만원으로 하향 조정한다. 상선 
시장 수주 경쟁 심화로 업종 밸류 조정이 이루어지고 있으나 동사는 엔진 기반 장기 
성장성으로 Peer 대비 상대적 매력도 확보 및 주가 방어가 가능하다는 판단이다.

Document 284: Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation

Source PDF: all report pdf/Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

Downgrade of global 
equities to a Core allocation  
Summary 
Event: We are downgrading global equities from Overweight 
to a Neutral/Core allocation. We continue to maintain a 
Neutral allocation to fixed income, Overweight on Gold and 
Underweight on Cash. There are more details in our Global 
Market Outlook publication on 30 October 2026. 
What does the equity downgrade mean?  
This downgrade implies a slightly lower allocation to global 
equities within a diversified portfolio. For our CIO Foundation 
Balanced portfolio, this would imply an equity allocation of 
52.5%, down from 53 .2%. We continue to expect positive 
returns for global equities  in the coming 6 -12 months , 
though equity investors may need to recalibrate expectations 
lower, compared with the 15 -20% annualised gains 
experienced in the last few years. Our capital market 
assumptions still suggest a mid -to-high single digit annual 
returns over the next few years, consistent with a transition 
to a mid -cycle bull market . Consensus estimates point to 
16% earnings growth for global equities in 2027, which 
should overcome various headwinds to deliver positive 
returns for equities over the next 12 months. Investors should 
thus, continue to maintain exposure to equities  within a 
diversified portfolio. 
In addition, we see a changing playbook  to prepare for 
higher volatility, more active management and greater 
diversification, as discussed below. 
What does it mean for regional equities 
positioning? 
Within a global equities portfolio, we remain Overweight US 
and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) equities , which are expected to 
deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 amongst the 
major regions. This growth is led by the technology sector, 
which carries the largest weight in both regions. Within the 
diversified equities portfolio, we maintain Core allocations to

Allocation to global equities decrease to 52.5% from 
53.2% for our CIO Foundation Balanced portfolio

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 30 October 2026

Euro area equities, which offer lower valuations, and Japan 
equities, which is backed by a reflating economy. We are 
Underweight UK equities, where the sector composition 
offers lower growth prospects in 2027. Within the AxJ region, 
we are Overweight Taiwan equities, with attractive structural 
growth driven by its semiconductor industry. We are also 
Overweight India equities, which offers diversified non-tech 
growth drivers. Taiwan and India equities both generate 
attractive returns on equity. We maintain Core allocations to 
China equities, with low valuations, and Korea equities, with 
explosive earnings growth in its semiconductors industry, but 
volatile price movements. We are Underweight ASEAN, 
which offers relatively lower earnings growth. 
What are the implications for equity sectors? 
We are Overweight these sectors globally: US 
information technology, US healthcare, US financials, US 
materials, Europe ex -UK financials, and Japan 
financials. 
A framework we use is to break the market into four key 
sector building blocks. Tech is the largest block, 
encompassing the Information Technology, Communication 
Services and Consumer Discretionary sectors. Put 
differently, the segment spans five major industries: 
semiconductors, hardware, internet, software, and IT 
services. Within the US market, we expect this segment to 
deliver c.30% earnings growth in 2027, extending the strong 
momentum seen in 2026. 
Financials and Real Estate  are the next building block, 
comprising these two sectors. We expect this segment to 
continue delivering low -teens earnings growth in 2027, 
supported by resilient economic activity and healthy credit 
trends. 
Resources include companies across four sectors: 
Industrials, Materials, Energy, and Utilities . We expect the 
segment to generate 10 -15% earnings growth in 2027, 
although Energy is likely to remain the largest drag on overall 
growth due to tougher comparisons and softer energy price 
assumptions for 2027. 
Healthcare & Others  consists primarily of Healthcare and 
Consumer Staples. We expect mid-teen earnings growth for 
the segment in 2027, with a rebound in healthcare earnings 
representing a potential upside surprise. 
Our Overweight sector exposures are drawn from each of the 
four key blocks above. Unlike the last few years , with 
concentrated gains from Tech providing market leadership, 
we see our downgrade in global equities also reflecting a 
broadening in market leadership towards each of the other 
sector blocks apart from Tech.

US and Asia ex-Japan would continue to lead in 
earnings growth in 2027

As structural themes such as AI transition from the rapid 
buildout phase towards monetisation, diversified exposure 
across sectors and regions should become increasingly 
important. 
What are the implications for equity 
opportunistic ideas? 
We have opportunistic ideas on Global High -Dividend 
equities, US healthcare, Euro area banks , MSCI 
Switzerland and Japan banks. The selection represents a 
more defensive stance  currently, with an average beta of 
below 1 relative to global equities (MSCI AC World), but 
attractive drivers nonetheless, for each of the ideas.  
Global High-Dividend offers higher-income equity allocation 
with historically lower volatility than global equities. US 
healthcare is supported by wider adoption of obesity 
treatments, advance s in oncology a nd innovative medical 
devices, that support earnings growth. Euro area banks  
continue to see steady earnings growth adding to their 
excess capital, which is being returned to shareholders via 
attractive dividend and share buyback yields. Japan banks 
continue to enjoy net margin expansion from BoJ policy 
normalisation, as well as corporate governance 
improvements. MSCI Switzerland is a newly initiated idea for 
investors seeking defensive exposure. H ealthcare and 
consumer staples account s for more than half the index 
weight, providing exposure to relatively resilient demand that 
is more insulated from economic cycles and AI trends. It also 
has a sizeable financial sector supported by steady wealth 
management growth and well-capitalised insurers with 
disciplined underwriting and resilient premium income. 
We see the case for more active management  in a mid -
cycle transition, as higher market dispersion should create 
greater opportunities for active sector and stock selection, 
particularly as leadership within equities evolves. 
What are the implications of higher equity 
market volatility?  
A more uncertain political backdrop and p otentially maturing 
growth rates in certain market segments may contribute to 
more frequent bouts of market volatility. Higher volatility 
presents an opportunity to use structured strategies to gain 
exposure to equities. Such strategies may be used to 
generate income or to gain exposure at a discounted entry 
price. Investors can also consider strategies that are 
structured to return principal or partial principal at maturity. 
What alternative strategies are suited to a 
regime of moderating equity market returns?  
Investors can consider alternative strategies such as hedge  
fund strategies or private markets, for those with the capacity

to accept some illiquidity.  
Equity long/short hedge fund  strategies would retain growth 
participation with less market exposure. Fund managers buy 
companies they expect to outperform and short those they 
expect to underperform, seeking to earn the difference 
between the two , and benefit even as total equity market 
returns are moderating. 
Within private markets , private equity provides  a means to 
higher returns with the effects of an illiquidity premium. 
Meanwhile, i nfrastructure offers  differentiated exposure to 
structural growth opportunities, where c ontracted long-term 
revenues reduce dependence on equity-market sentiment. 
What are the implications on fixed income 
and FX markets? 
Bonds: This year’s surge in bond yields means bonds now 
offer a hedge against any equity market weakness. We 
believe the 3 -7-year part of the US bond yield curve offers 
the best hedge against any equity sell -off caused by AI -
related growth worries. This part of the bond curve has priced 
in most Fed rate hike expectations, which could reverse if 
growth concerns return. Meanwhile, we favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a hedge against any 
fuel price-led inflation scare.  
Credit: For corporate bonds, adopting a defensive stance in 
a volatile environment typically involves: i) increasing credit 
quality, favouring higher-rated issuers; and ii) moving up the 
capital structure, by reducing exposure to subordinated debt 
and favouring secured debt. From a sector perspective, 
large, diversified banks, utilities, and quasi -sovereigns tend 
to be more defensive, particularly relative to cyclical sectors, 
such as metals and mining, exploration, and technology.         
FX: We favour bearish AUD/JPY as a tactical hedge against 
any equity market correction. Review of 21 past S&P500 
correction sessions (2010 -2025) shows AUD weakened 
against USD in 86% of cases, while JPY strengthened 
against USD in 67%, supporting our bearish AUD/JPY 
outlook during any equity market stress.

Document 301: First Impressions_ NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change of OCR call (2026-10-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change of OCR call (2026-10-30).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change
of OCR call
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:19 October 22 2026 Share
Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter and 3.7% y/y. Core inflation
pressures are elevated and market pricing is primed for a 25bp OCR hike next week, which
we now expect.
Consumers Price Index, September quarter 2026 – key stats

### Page 2

Headline inflation
Quarterly change: +0.7% (prev: +1.5%)
Westpac forecast: +0.7%
RBNZ: +0.8%
Market median: +0.8%, range +0.5% to +1.2%

Annual change: +3.7% (prev: +4.1%)
Westpac forecast: +3.7%, RBNZ: +3.9%, Market: +3.8%

Non-tradables
Quarterly change: +1.0% (prev: +0.6%)
Westpac forecast: +1.3%
Annual change: +3.3% (prev: +3.4%)

Tradables
Quarterly change: +0.2% (prev: +2.7%)
Westpac forecast: -0.2%
Annual change: +4.3% (prev: +4.9%)

Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter. That saw the annual inflation rate easing to
3.7%, down from 4.1% in the year to June. 
Today’s result was in line with our forecast, but lower than the RBNZ’s forecast for 3.9% annual
inflation. 
However, under the surface, the details were more concerning. Measure of core inflation have taken a
step higher, with several closely watched measures now at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s
target band. 
We suspect that today’s result will leave the market continuing to price a strong chance of another
OCR hike at next Wednesday’s policy review. In that light, we think the RBNZ will regard this as the
path of least resistance, and we now expect that they will deliver (previously we expected that they
would pause and assess, before hiking at the December MPS).

What contributed to inflation in the September quarter?
Underpinning September’s 0.7% rise in consumer prices were some big price movements in both
directions. 
The largest move in prices this quarter was actually a sharp fall in fuel costs. Fuel prices aren’t low by
any stretch of the imagination. However, average prices for both petrol and diesel over the September
quarter were lower than in the June quarter when prices rocketed higher in the wake of the Middle East

### Page 3

conflict. In terms of specifics, the average price for 91 unleaded was 6.5% lower in September than in
the June quarter, while diesel prices were close to 16% lower. Those moves took 0.4ppts off inflation
this quarter. 
While prices at the pump weren’t as high as they were in the June quarter, fuel costs are still painfully
high. And that’s been bleeding through into higher prices for other goods and services. Of note, the
September quarter saw large increases in passenger transport costs, with international airfares up 14%
and domestic airfares up nearly 8%. 
The September quarter also saw the annual increases in local council rates (up 6.2%) and charges for
services like water supply (up 7.5%). That continues the run of large increases seen in recent years.
Lastly, the past few months saw a 1.0% rise in food prices, largely as a result of the seasonal rise in
prices for fresh vegetables. 
On the downside, we’ve seen continued softness in housing rents, which are the largest component of
the CPI, accounting for 11.2% of the basket. With low population growth and ample supply, rents were
flat in the September quarter and have effectively been flat since the start of this year. In annual terms,
rental inflation is the lowest it’s been in more than two decades, with regions like Wellington seeing
outright falls.

Annual and core inflation 
The annual inflation rate dropped back to 3.7% in the September quarter, down from 4.1% in the year
to June and the highest level in two years.
Looking under the surface, prices in the domestically oriented non-tradables group rose 1.0% over the
quarter, less than expected. That saw annual non-tradables inflation nudging down to 3.3% (from 3.4%
in the year to June). 
Non-tradables inflation has continued to run at above-average rates over the past year. That’s despite
the softness in economic growth and the labour market. In large part, that’s due to the continued
sizeable increases in administered prices, such as electricity charges which rose 9.3% over the past
year. 
However, prices in other areas have also been firm. Non-tradables excluding housing and utilities costs
(which also omits local council rates) was 3.4% for the year. In seasonally adjusted terms, quarterly
non-tradables inflation has been tracking around 0.8% for most of the past year. It was noted that in
sectors like construction, higher materials costs are adding to prices. Feedback from business we've
heard indicates that those higher materials costs are likely related to high oil prices. 
On the imported front, lower fuel costs saw tradable prices rising by just 0.2% over the September
quarter (though above our forecast for a small fall). That saw annual tradables inflation dropping back
to 4.3%, down sharply from the 4.9% rise in the year to June. 
While fuel costs have been a key driver for tradables inflation, under the surface, other imported
inflation pressures are looking firm. Excluding food and fuel costs, tradables prices were up 2.7% over
the past year. That’s the highest level in three years. 
With big swings in fuel costs, and concern that this is spilling over into operating costs more generally,
a key focus for the RBNZ is what’s been happening to measures of core inflation (which smooth
through quarter-to-quarter swings in individual prices and instead track the underlying trend in

### Page 4

Related articles
inflation). 
On this front we did some see worrying signs. Several closely watched measures of core inflation have
picked up, and now sit at the top of (or slightly above) the RBNZ target band. In terms of specifics, we
saw: 
Reinforcing this picture of firm underlying inflation pressures, a growing number of goods have been
increasing in price. That could be an early indication that businesses, even those with a limited
exposure to fuel costs, are starting to factor in expectations of continued high inflation when setting
prices.

Outlook 
While today’s result was lower than the RBNZ assumed in their September MPS projections, the
firmness in core inflation will give the RBNZ pause for thought ahead of their policy review on 28
October. Furthermore, with a sharp rise in petrol prices in recent weeks, September’s easing in annual
inflation is likely to be just a temporary reprieve. We expect headline inflation to reaccelerate back over
4% before the end of this year, and it’s set to remain above the RBNZ’s current forecast for most of
2027. 
Market pricing and views have coalesced around an October hike which we now suspect the MPC will
see as the path of least resistance. Core inflation measures are now close to or above 3%, which will
add fuel to arguments from the MPC hawks. We will have more detail in our MPR Preview later today,
including on the implications for the shape of the tightening cycle moving forward.

CPI ex-fuel inflation: +3.0% yr (vs +2.9% previously)
CPI ex-fuel and food: 3.2% yr (vs +2.9% previously)
CPI ex-fuel, food and household energy: +2.9% yr (vs +2.5% previously)
30% trimmed mean: +3.0% (vs +2.6% previously)
Weighted median: +3.1% (vs +2.7% previously)

Padhraic Garvey, CFA, Michiel Tukker and Benjamin Schroeder

that overblown, and we land in a less dramatic place? We explore

PROPRIETARY RESEARCH

STOXX 600 Earnings Outlook provides analysis and commentary on aggregate earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.

Analyst: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA                                           October 29, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com

STOXX 600 EARNINGS OUTLOOK  
EUROPEAN AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS

 Third quarter earnings are expected to increase 23.6% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, earnings 
are expected to increase 9.8%.

 Third quarter revenue is expected to increase 13.6% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, revenues are 
expected to increase 6.2%.

 144 companies in the STOXX 600 have reported earnings to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 54.2% reported results 
exceeding analyst estimates.

 180 companies in the STOXX 600 have reported revenue to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 61.1% reported revenue 
exceeding analyst estimates.

 During the week of November 2, 65 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

Through October 29, 144 companies in the STOXX 600 Index reported earnings for Q3 2026. Of these, 54.2% reported 
earnings above analyst estimates. In a typical quarter (sin ce 2012), 54% of companies beat estimates and 39% miss 
estimates.

In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 5.0% above estimates, which is above the 5.9% long-term 
(since 2012) average surprise factor.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the STOXX 600 for Q3 2026 is 23.6%. The STOXX 600 expects to see share-
weighted earnings of €162.6B in Q3 2026, compared to shar e-weighted earnings of €131.5B (based on the year-ago 
earnings of the current constituents) in Q3 2025.

Eight of the ten sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to Q3 2025. The Energy sector 
has the highest earnings growth rate for the quarter, wh ile the Real Estate has the weakest anticipated growth 
compared to Q3 2025.

The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (139%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €33.6B in Q3 2026, 
compared to earnings of €14.1B in Q3 2025.

The Real Estate sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (-69%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €1B in Q3 2026, 
compared to earnings of €3.1B in Q3 2025.

ANALYST: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA October 30, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com; 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
 26Q3 Y/Y earnings are expected to be 31.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the Y/Y earnings estimate is 26.4%. 
 Of the 299 companies in the S&P 500 that have reported earnings to date for 26Q3, 81.6% have reported earnings 
above analyst estimates. This compares to a long-term average of 67.7% and prior four quarter average of 81.5%. 
 26Q3 Y/Y revenue is expected to be 13.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the growth estimate is 12.2%. 
 69.9% of companies have reported 26Q3 revenue above analyst expectations. This compares to a long-term 
average of 63% and an average over the past four quarters of 76.8%. 
 For 26Q3, there have been 40 negative EPS preannouncements issued by S&P 500 corporations compared to 66 
positive EPS preannouncements. By dividing 40 by 66 the N/P ratio is 0.6 for the S&P 500 Index.  
 The forward four-quarter (26Q4– 27Q3) P/E ratio for the S&P 500 is 19.0. 
 During the week of Nov. 2, 144 S&P 500 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings. 
SECTION A: EARNINGS OUTLOOK 
Q3 2026: EARNINGS REVISIONS

PROPRIETARY RESEARCH FROM LSEG OCTOBER 30, 2026 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
This Week in Earnings provides analysis and commentary on aggregate earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.  2 
There has been an increase in the share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 since the start of the quarter (to $778.4B 
from $760.7B). Three of the eleven sectors have experienced downward revisions to estimates.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-20.5%) and materials (-7%) sectors have recorded the highest percentage 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (33%) sector has recorded the highest percentage increase in earnings. 
Overall, share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 2.3% since the start of the quarter.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-14.7B) and materials (-1.2B) sectors have recorded the highest dollar-level 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (15.7B) sector has recorded the highest dollar-level increase in earnings. 
Overall, expected share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 17.7B since the start of the quarter.

299 companies in the S&P 500 Index have reported earnings for Q3 2026. Of these companies, 81.6% reported 
earnings above analyst expectations and 15.7% reported earnings below analyst expectations. In a typical quarter 
(since 1994), 67% of companies beat estimates and 20% miss estimates. Over the past four quarters, 82% of 
companies beat the estimates and 15% missed estimates. 
In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 4.5% above estimates, which compares to a long-term (since 
1994) average surprise factor of 4.4% and the average surprise factor over the prior four quarters of 7.7%.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 31.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 26.4%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $778.4B in 26Q3, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $591.4B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 503 constituents) in 25Q3. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q3. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Consumer 
Discretionary sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (146.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $63B in 26Q3, 
compared to earnings of $25.6B in 25Q3. 4 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (566.5%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (143.8%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 39%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (68.9%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $260.2B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $154B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (137%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (71.5%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 28.2%. 
The Consumer Discretionary sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.9%) of any sector. It is expected to earn 
$56.3B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $54.2B in 25Q3. 7 of the 18 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to 
see lower earnings than a year ago. The Casinos & Gaming (-44.1%) and Automotive Parts & Equipment (-38.8%) 
sub-industries have the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate 
improves to 4.5%.

the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q4. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (145.6%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $59.5B in 26Q4, 
compared to earnings of $24.2B in 25Q4. 3 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (366.3%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (175.7%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 52.1%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (56.3%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $292.1B in 26Q4, compared to earnings of $186.8B in 25Q4. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (119.5%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (72.3%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 19.1%. 
The Industrials sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.9%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $52B in 26Q4, 
compared to earnings of $50.1B in 25Q4. 2 of the 20 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower earnings 
than a year ago. The Aerospace & Defense (-39.6%) and Passenger Airlines (-29.8%) sub-industries have the lowest 
earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate improves to 30.5%.

STARMINE EARNINGS SURPRISE FORECAST
Looking forward at quarterly performance, we use StarMine’s SmartEstimate® from LSEG to determine which
companies in the S&P 500 are better poised to beat and miss earnings estimates. The SmartEstimate® is a weighted

average of analyst estimates, with more weight given to more recent estimates and more accurate analysts. Our
studies have shown that when the SmartEstimate® differs significantly from the consensus (I/B/E/S mean estimate),
the Predicted Surprise accurately predicts the direction of earnings surprises or further revisions 70% of the time.
When a significant Predicted Surprise for revenue is also present for the period, the accuracy improves to 78%.

StarMine ARM from LSEG is an analyst revisions stock ranking model, designed to predict future changes in analyst
sentiment. ARM incorporates more accurate earnings estimates through the SmartEstimate prediction service. ARM
region rankings scores companies by region on a scale of 1 to 100 where 100 represents the most bullish sentiment.
Over the next two weeks, 147 S&P 500 companies are expected to report earnings. Of these companies, 17 positive

surprises and 11 negative surprises are expected from S&P 500 companies reporting quarterly results.

Document 348: Open mouth operations (2026-10-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Open mouth operations (2026-10-30).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
Open mouth operations
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
13:34 October 23 2026 Share
The RBA does not want to promise what it will do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks.
Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for every nuance. Lately, there seems to have
been more RBA communication to pore over. The method of delivery has also evolved. This has

### Page 2

Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks for clues to future rate moves. They will
have plenty to pore over in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry of fireside chats
this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday.
Next Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before Senate Estimates, just as the
quarterly CPI is released. (This timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After the

decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a media release and press conference. More
speeches will follow, including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just following the RBA Review but also more
recently. There may be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication outside that certainly
feels more frequent than a couple of years ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little

sparse outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary appearances. We have counted
58 public engagements so far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website. This compares
with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in 2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events were related to economics, markets
and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed as well. There are fewer set-piece

speeches on policy and a lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few years ago, there
are more interviews across a wide range of media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It
was a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the 7:30 Report; it is hard to imagine him
agreeing to an Assistant Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general audience. Central banks want to explain

why they do what they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising but the household
sector is not the source of the inflation.
Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief
Communication Officer highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting media conference is
designed to speak to the public. Market participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
sometimes caused confusion when tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different tone and

varying complexity.
The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly using speeches directed to professional
audiences to discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such as neutral rates and
financial conditions. They are also highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more ways
than just squeezing mortgage holders.
One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises

about the future path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running commentary about how it
is interpreting recent data. These interpretations are themselves useful signals about what the RBA
thinks it might do.

### Page 3

the primary audience. So if you think that what the Governor said in the media conference sounded
more ‘dovish’ than what another official says in a more market-oriented forum, it is partly because she
is trying to convey that the RBA understands that its recent policy actions are making things harder for
many Australians. But this is in much the same way that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It
does, but that will not stop the treatment.

A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted material such as the minutes and set-
piece speeches than in unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be careful with their
words in unscripted settings. If the tone differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have reduced the pressure to make the set-piece

speeches more accessible. Research on central bank communication often uses measures such as
the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was
beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison suggests four speeches from 2026 (1, 2, 3,
4) were harder to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches scored in the 11 to 12
range, which is high-school-level complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and 15, which is

academic or technically dense text requiring university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-Governor Phil Lowe also scored better
on these metrics than the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that readability metrics do
not necessarily imply better clarity if the concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has

begun to put more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those invisible ‘star’ variables
(NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences
has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on the full range of transmission channels from
interest rates to inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour supply channel). This is

understandable in a world of supply shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt they would be comforted, though, by the
Deputy Governor’s explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the smallest of the
transmission channels from interest rates to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on inflation. It raises the question of whether

other policy tools or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on inflation through the other
channels without hitting a minority of households disproportionately.
We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases to summarise the position. From ‘narrow
path’ to ‘not ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once entrenched, a phrase can
outlast its usefulness.

### Page 4

Related articles
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record the presentations to the Board and the
members’ discussion. The minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-meeting
statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The
August 2024 minutes were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how sensitive the forecasts
were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed

Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s communication have not changed. It
wants to explain – and perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how it interprets
incoming data, especially inflation, the labour market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ about
future moves. Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular future path for interest

rates. Some academic literature argues that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high, for
that matter) independently amplifies the transmission of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-
period interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield curve. Central banks including the RBA
have certainly tried jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view, though, is that the
potential cost of reputational loss if things do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a

slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides the signal about future policy directions. This
is why it was noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour market as ‘a little tight’ after the
September meeting, even though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We have been
noting for some time that the RBA staff have a downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the

RBA’s oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not
surprise us.

United States: Elevated inflation and high growth
02/10/2026 4
August 2026: Marked improvement in services
business confidence, from an already high level
September 2026: Manufacturing business

confidence remains stable at a high level
August 2026: Increasing inflationary pressures September 2026: Supplier deliveries do not get

U.S. macroeconomic performance has remained solid since the onset of the shock, as illustrated in particular by Q2 growth
of +3.8% and +9% (annualized quarterly rate) in consumer spending and private nonresidential investment, respectively.

In the manufacturing sector, business sentiment has gained momentum despite the shock, while the impact of this shock
on the services sector has remained very limited. The immediate reaction of consumer confidence was, however, more
negative before beginning to improve, which was nevertheless interrupted in August (latest available data for households’
sentiment on their future personal financial situation). The University of Michigan’s composite indicator continued to

deteriorate in September, due to the sharp rise in fuel prices. But declining confidence still does not translate into reduced
spending—at least not at the aggregate level.
On the inflation front, the spike immediately following the outbreak of the conflict in Iran was more pronounced than in
the months following Russia’s invasion of Ukraine. The decline in June still left inflation at a high level, but its stability in
July and August was notable (3.4% y/y), as was the latest—the third consecutive—slight decline in inflation excluding

energy in August (2.5% y/y, same rate as in February). Given the existing inflationary pressures, however, inflation is highly
likely to resume its upward trend in September.

Document 400: US Economy Continues To Roar, While The Fed Waits For More

Source PDF: all report pdf/US Economy Continues To Roar, While The Fed Waits For More.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 9

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US Economy Continues To Roar, While The Fed Waits For More
Date:    4 hours ago
US Economy Continues To Roar, While The Fed Waits For More

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US Economy Continues To Roar,
While The Fed Waits For More
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 29 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Pawan Sharma / Unsplash

### Page 2

The FOMC voted 10-2 on Wednesday to hold the federal funds rate at
3.75%-4.00%, with the two dissenters favoring a 25bps hike. Yet Fed
Chair Kevin Warsh's assessment of the US economy sounded decidedly
hawkish. Economic growth is robust and broadening, the labor market is
in good shape, inflation remains above the Fed's 2.0% y/y target, and
financial conditions are hardly tight. So why did the Fed stand pat?
Warsh argued that September's hike was already tightening financial

conditions, inflation expectations remained anchored, and stronger
productivity growth was allowing the economy to expand without
overheating. With an "information-rich" period ahead, the Fed saw little
reason to rush, he said.
In our view, the decision was puzzling. It reminds us of someone standing
in a room with a fire alarm blaring and smoke pouring under the door, yet
insisting on more evidence before calling the fire department. Thursday's
economic data only reinforced our conviction that the Fed should have

raised rates by 25bps on Wednesday. Let's take a closer look.
(1) GDP. The US economy expanded at a 3.4% annualized rate in Q3-
2026, the largest gain in a year (chart).
A closer look reveals remarkably strong underlying demand. Real final
sales to private domestic purchasers, excluding volatile trade and
inventory swings, surged 4.9% (saar), the best increase since Q2-2021
(chart)! Consumer spending advanced 3.8% for the second consecutive
quarter, its fastest pace since Q4-2024. Nonresidential fixed investment

### Page 3

soared 12.7%, a pace last exceeded outside the pandemic period in Q2-
1978. The AI investment boom was a major driver, with investment in
information-processing equipment surging 37.4% and software investment
rising 9.6%. Yet headline GDP understated this strength because net
exports subtracted 3.32 percentage points from growth. Imports surged
19.7%, led by AI-related durable goods. Exports fell 5.7%.
Consumer spending and business investment are driving the US

economic boom. Federal government spending is too, rising 15.8% last
quarter.
The Weekly Economic Index rose to 3.3% for the week ended October 16,
signaling a solid start to Q4's real GDP growth (chart).
(2) Inflation. In Q3's GDP report, core PCED inflation remained
troublesome at 3.0% y/y, well above the Fed's 2.0% target (chart). On the

### Page 4

brighter side, it rose at an annualized rate of 2.4% during the quarter.
Headline PCED inflation rose 0.5% m/m in September, lifting its yearly
rate to 3.6% (chart). Core PCED inflation, excluding food and energy,
increased 0.3% m/m and 3.1% y/y. Both remain stubbornly above the
Fed's inflation target. Furthermore, Warsh's inflation diffusion measure
shows that nearly half of PCED components rose more than 3.0% y/y,
compared with a pre-pandemic average of just 32%.

Goods PCED inflation rose to 3.9% y/y in September from 3.6% in August,
partly reflecting a 7.3% m/m increase in gasoline prices (chart).

### Page 5

Tariff-related price pressures have yet to subside, while the AI investment
boom is adding to inflation across the technology sector. Prices for
computers and software are rising at record annual rates (chart). Together,
these forces suggest goods inflation is unlikely to ease meaningfully soon.
Services PCED inflation rose to 3.5% y/y in September (chart). Supercore
services inflation, excluding housing and energy, edged up to 3.6% y/y
(chart). It has been trending higher since late 2025, underscoring

persistent underlying price pressures.

### Page 6

Services inflation remains broad-based, with most major categories rising
around 3.0% y/y or more. Transportation services inflation has been
particularly troublesome as higher energy costs feed through to service
prices (chart).
(3) Consumer spending and income. Americans continue to shop. Real
consumer spending remained at a record high in September, even as real
disposable personal income (DPI) declined for the first time since April
(chart). Real DPI has been essentially flat for over a year, a trend likely to

persist as more Baby Boomers retire.

### Page 7

September's saving rate declined to 3.8%, the lowest since 2022 (chart).
We expect it to fall further as retiring Baby Boomers continue spending
their sizeable net worth because they are no longer earning labor income.
Real consumer spending was flat in September, as a 0.3% m/m decline in
goods purchases offset a 0.2% increase in services spending. Spending
on restaurants and hotels remained particularly robust, rising 0.5%
following a 1.1% surge in August (chart).

### Page 8

(4) Labor market. Initial unemployment claims fell to just 194,000 during
the week of October 24, marking the tenth reading below 200,000 this
year, the most in any year since 1969! The four-week moving average
also fell to its lowest level since October 1969. Continuing claims also
declined, approaching their lowest level since April 2023. These figures
underscore the labor market's remarkable resilience (chart).

leave comments or questions at the end of our QuickTakes, and it
may be featured in the next "Just Ask Dr. Ed!"

聯電 2303 TT  ( 電子–半導體 ) 
聯年攀升，價量齊揚 
投資與評價 
1. 聯電 3Q26 受惠成熟製程及 AI 相關需求回升，晶圓出貨季增 7.3%，稼
動率攀升至 92%，本業創近三年新高；4Q26 隨新加坡 Fab 12i 產能持
續開出，22nm 新產品及矽光子業務逐步貢獻，稼動率維持 90%以上。 
2. 聯電 26 年受惠成熟製程需求回升 、 新加坡Fab 12i 產能開出及稼動率提
升，EPS 7.33 元；27 年成熟製程供需趨緊，加上矽光子、TFLN、DTC
及先進封裝等高附加價值業務逐步貢獻 ，EPS 8.35 元， 營運創歷史新高。 
3. 目前聯電交易在 128 元，位於近三年參考本益比區間 9~22x 平均值位
置，考量成熟製程復甦加快，稼動率攀升，且先進封裝與矽光子將開始
貢獻營收，維持買進投資建議，因上調27 年獲利預估，本益比由平均值
之 16 倍調高至中間偏上之 18 倍，目標價由 130 元(16X 修正前 27(F) 
PER)，調升至 150 元(18X 修正後 27(F) PER)。 
重點更新 
成熟製程需求提升使聯電 3Q26 稼動率達 92%，本業創三近年新高 
聯電 3Q26 受惠於成熟製程供需結構持續改善，AI 相關 PMIC、通訊與消費
性電子需求回升 ， 整體產能利用率由2Q26 的 85%提升至 92%，但因漲價幅
度略低於原先預期，聯電 3Q26 營收 742.39 億元(+8.0% QoQ) ，略低於預
期。隨著產能利用率提升 ， 毛利率由前一季之32.5%提升至 34.0%(+1.5ppts 
QoQ)，營業利益 186.43 億元(+29.3% QoQ) ，惟 2Q26 因權益法認列較高
投資收益，3Q26 業外收支淨額由前一季之 302.36 億元降至 12.68 億元，稅
後淨利為 163.89 億元(-61.2% QoQ)，EPS 1.32 元，本業為三近年新高。 
預估聯電 4Q26 AI 需求支撐晶圓出貨成長，產能利用率維持九成以上 
隨著 AI 相關電源管理、FPGA 及高速連接晶片需求持續成長，加上成熟製程
供需逐步趨緊 ， 新加坡Fab 12i 產能持續擴充 ， 配合22nm 新產品逐步量產，
以及矽光子相關業務開始貢獻營收，但因 3Q26 產品價格調漲幅度低於預
期，故下修 4Q26 混合 ASP 預估，小幅下調聯電 4Q26 營收 1.5%至 777.49
億元(+4.7% QoQ)。儘管部分 PC 相關 LCD 控制 IC、DDIC 及 Wi-Fi 需求出
現放緩，但 AI 相關高附加價值產品占比提升，維持產能利用率 90%出頭之
預估，惟混合 ASP 調低影響毛利率表現，調降毛利率預估至 34.6%。下調稅
後淨利至 166.90 億元(+1.8% QoQ)，EPS 1.34 元。

### Page 2

報告內容 
成熟製程需求提升使聯電 3Q26 稼動率達 92%，本業創近三年新高 
聯電受惠於成熟製程供需結構持續改善，以及AI 相關電源管理、通訊與消費性電子需求回升，
3Q26 晶圓出貨量達 121.2 萬片 12 吋約當晶圓(+7.3% QoQ)， 創歷史新高 ， 整體產能利用率由
2Q26 的 85%提升至 92%，其中多座 12 吋晶圓廠已達滿載，8 吋產線亦隨客戶訂單回流而明
顯復甦 。 此外22/28nm 製程營收再創單季新高 ， 其中22nm 營收占晶圓銷售比重提升至 18.3%，
主要受惠於 DDIC、車用雷達及 Wi-Fi IC 等新產品持續導入，帶動晶圓出貨成長，本季針對非
長約客戶有進行部分漲價，推算平均漲價幅度約 5~6%，但由於 8 吋的產能利用率提升幅度大
於 12 吋，且 8 吋的 ASP 低於 12 吋，使得本季的混合 ASP 持平於前一季。 
因漲價幅度略低於原先預期，聯電 3Q26 營收 742.39 億元(+8.0% QoQ) ，略低於預期。隨著
產能利用率提升 ， 有效分攤晶圓製造固定成本 ， 且高附加價值22nm 及特殊製程產品持續增加，
抵銷部分折舊費用增加影響，毛利率由前一季之 32.5%提升至 34.0%(+1.5ppts QoQ)。在管理
及研發費用季減，以及其他營業收益增加帶動下，營業費用降至65.62 億元(-17.0% QoQ)，推
升營業利益至 186.43 億元(+29.3% QoQ)，營業利益率 25.1%。惟 2Q26 因權益法認列較高投
資收益 ， 使業外收支基期偏高 ，3Q26 營業外收支淨額由前一季之 302.36 億元降至 12.68 億元，
所得稅費用增加，稅後淨利為 163.89 億元(-61.2% QoQ)，EPS 1.32 元，本業為近三年新高。

晶圓出貨與稼動率雙升，預期聯電 26 年營運創歷史新高 
聯電 26 年受惠於成熟製程供需環境改善，AI 相關需求持續帶動電源管理、FPGA 及高速連接
晶片成長，配合 22/28nm 製程在 DDIC、車用雷達及 Wi-Fi 等應用的新產品持續導入，相較
25 年產能利用率受消費性電子需求疲弱及成熟製程庫存調整影響，26 年自第二季起晶圓出貨
逐季回升，整體產能利用率由 1Q26 的 79%提升至 3Q26 的 92%，第四季亦預期維持 90%以
上，其中 22nm 製程營收占比持續提升，顯示高價值製程需求逐步增強。此外聯電積極布局矽
光子、TFLN 及先進封裝等新技術，26 年已與 SILITH 達成矽光子產品量產里程碑，並與國內其
他廠商合作開發 AI 伺服器應用 DTC 深溝槽電容，相關技術逐步由產品驗證轉向商業化。 
聯電新加坡 Fab 12i 產能也逐步開出，與 Intel 合作之 12nm 專案同樣進展順利，預計 26 年提
供 PDK 與 IP，應用涵蓋 Wi-Fi、數位電視與高速介面，惟推算 3Q26 產品價格調漲幅度低於原
先預期，小幅下修 26 年營收預估 1.2%至 2,817.58 億元(+18.6% YoY)。因 3Q26 混合 ASP 低
於預期及下調 4Q26 混合 ASP 預估 ， 小幅調低毛利率預估 1.3 個百分點至 32.7%(+3.7ppts YoY)。
調降營業利益預估 3.9%至 620.62 億元(+47.9% YoY)， 稅後淨利914.20 億元(+120.1% YoY)，
EPS 7.33 元，營運創歷史新高。

### Page 4

預期聯電 27 年 ASP 調漲與產品組合優化，推升營運再創新高 
預期聯電 27 年主要成長動能將由 26 年之出貨量與產能利用率提升逐步轉向 ASP 調整及高附
加價值產品組合改善，隨著 AI 基礎建設持續擴大，電源管理 IC、FPGA 及高速連接晶片需求維
持強勁，加上部分 8 吋及 12 吋製程已接近滿載，成熟製程供需結構持續趨緊，有利聯電與客
戶進行價格協商，預估 1Q27 混合 ASP 季增 7~8%。22nm 製程持續受惠於客戶新產品量產及
晶圓代工滲透率提升，矽光子則隨 12 吋 PIC 平台支援 1.6T 光通訊應用，以及 TFLN 高速調變
器與先進封裝整合技術逐步成熟，開始增加營收貢獻。雖然新增矽光子、DTC 及 TFLN 產能主
要於 28 年底後陸續開出 ， 使27 年晶圓出貨成長受到既有產能限制 ， 但預期透過製程瓶頸改善、
既有產能重新配置，以及高附加價值產品比重提升，仍可持續推升營運規模。 
上修 27 年營收預估 0.3%至 3,689.23 億元(+30.9% YoY)。在 ASP 改善、22nm 及特殊製程產
品組合優化，以及產能利用率維持 90%以上的帶動下，預期單位晶圓產值提高，且高稼動率有
助於分攤固定製造成本 ，上調毛利率預估 0.3 個百分點至 41.5%(+8.7ppts YoY)。 隨著矽光子、
TFLN、DTC 及 Hybrid Bonding 等技術持續開發，以及新加坡 P4 與台南 P7、P8 擴產計畫逐
步推進，相關研發、人力及管理費用將隨業務規模擴大而增加，故預估營業費用年增 37.9%至
416.00 億元，調升營業利益預估 1.0%至 1,113.59 億元(+79.4% YoY)，稅後淨利 1,038.76 億
元(+13.6% YoY)，EPS 8.35 元，營運再創歷史新高。

### Page 5

成熟製程需求改善及高價值特殊製程逐步放量，聯電稼動率維持高檔 
聯電短期受惠成熟製程供需結構改善，AI 相關電源管理、FPGA 及連接晶片需求持續成長，帶
動 8 吋與 12 吋產能利用率同步提升，3Q26 整體稼動率達 92%，部分製程已接近滿載。展望
27 年，隨產能供給趨緊及高附加價值產品占比提升，營運成長動能將由出貨量增加轉向 ASP
改善與產品組合優化，中長期則持續深化 22nm 特殊製程、矽光子、TFLN、DTC 及先進封裝
布局 ， 並透過新加坡與台南雙軌擴產計畫 ， 加速AI 相關產能建置 ， 其中12 吋 PIC 與 8 吋 TFLN
平台已支援 1.6T 高速光通訊應用，預計 27 年逐步增加營收貢獻，隨 28 年底後新增產能陸續
開出，進一步推升AI 相關業務占比，帶動公司由傳統成熟製程代工轉向高附加價值特殊製程及
異質整合平台。 
目前聯電股價交易在 128 元，位於近三年參考本益比區間 9~22x 平均值位置，考量成熟製程
復甦加快，稼動率攀升，且先進封裝與矽光子將開始貢獻營收，維持買進投資建議，因上調 27
年獲利預估，本益比由平均值之 16 倍調高至中間偏上之 18 倍，目標價由 130 元(16X 修正前
27(F) PER)調升至 150 元(18X 修正後 27(F) PER)。

### Page 6

公司簡介 
聯電是一家專注於純晶圓代工的半導體公司 ， 其核心競爭力並不在於先進製程節點的技術領先 ，
而是在成熟製程的量產能力、良率穩定度、成本控制與長期客戶關係。公司長期深耕 28 奈米
以上製程，並在顯示驅動 IC、影像感測、嵌入式非揮發記憶體、高壓與功率元件等特色製程平
台建立規模優勢。從產業定位來看，聯電更接近景氣循環型但具結構性支撐的公司，而非高度
技術競賽導向的成長型公司。

在獲利結構上，聯電的關鍵驅動因子主要來自稼動率與產品組合的變化。成熟製程的固定成本
比重高，因此當整體需求回溫、產能利用率上升時，毛利率通常會呈現明顯改善。相反地，若
需求疲弱導致稼動率下滑，即使營收跌幅有限，獲利仍可能受到放大衝擊。此外，營收成長的
品質也高度依賴產品組合，來自特色製程或車用、工控平台的訂單，對平均售價與毛利率的貢
獻，明顯優於一般消費性成熟製程產品。

成熟製程市場本身具有較強的商品化特性，價格與供需循環對聯電的短中期表現影響甚大。相
較先進製程由少數廠商主導，成熟製程更容易受到同業擴產、價格競爭以及客戶庫存調整的影
響。因此，在研究聯電時，不能僅以公司自身營運為出發點，而必須同時評估整體成熟製程產
能是否出現結構性過剩，以及終端市場是否進入補庫存或去庫存階段。

圖 2. Revenue by Application

資料來源: UMC、國泰證期研究部整理

在中長期結構上，車用與工業應用被視為聯電相對穩定的成長支柱。這類應用具備產品生命週
期長 、 轉換成本高與品質要求嚴格等特性 ， 有助於降低價格波動與需求急劇反轉的風險 。 然而，
其導入與認證週期較長，對短期財務數字的拉動效果有限，因此更適合作為評估聯電估值下緣
與營運穩定度的依據，而非短線成長動能。

### Page 7

聯電 ESG 政策 
聯華電子股份有限公司以創造以人為本、與環境共生、與社會共榮的全球性友善生態新價值作
為永續發展的核心願景，將環境(Environmental)、社會(Social)與公司治理(Governance)全面
整合至公司營運策略與決策架構之中 。 聯電認為 ， 企業長期競爭力不僅來自於技術與製造能力，
更奠基於穩健治理、負責任的環境管理，以及對員工、供應鏈與社會的正向影響。基於此，聯
電持續透過制度化的 ESG 政策與行動方案，確保公司在追求營運成長的同時，能有效降低對
環境與社會所造成的衝擊，並回應利害關係人之期待。

1. 公司治理： 
在公司治理面向，聯電以董事會作為  ESG 治理之最高層級，負責督導永續策略方向與重大決
策。董事會下設之永續發展暨提名委員會由全體獨立董事組成，專責監督  ESG 治理成效，而 
ESG 指導委員會則由共同總經理擔任主席，結合多位核心高階主管，負責永續策略目標的訂定
與執行。透過此一由上而下的治理體系，聯電確保永續議題能夠實質納入公司策略、資本配置
與績效管理之中，並定期向董事會報告執行成果，使  ESG 不僅停留於政策宣示，而是成為公
司治理的一部分。 
圖 3. ESG 治理組織架構圖

資料來源:UMC、國泰證期研究部整理

2. 風險治理與誠信經營： 
在風險治理與誠信經營方面，聯電將  ESG 議題全面納入企業風險管理架構，依循  COSO 企
業風險管理原則，系統性辨識、評估並管理營運過程中可能產生的治理、環境與社會風險。企
業風險管理委員會負責跨部門整合風險資訊，並與  ESG 指導委員會保持密切連結，確保氣候
變遷、供應鏈、人權、資訊安全等重大永續風險，能即時反映於公司決策與因應策略之中。此
一治理設計，使聯電能在高度變動的產業環境中，維持營運穩健與長期韌性。 
圖 4. 企業風險管理組織架構圖

資料來源:UMC、國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 8

3. 環境政策： 
在環境政策方面，聯電承諾以低碳轉型與資源效率為核心，朝 2050 年淨零排放目標邁進，並
積極響應 RE100 再生能源倡議。公司持續推動氣候行動，透過製程改善、設備升級與能源結
構轉型，有效降低溫室氣體排放。至 2024 年，聯電範疇一與範疇二溫室氣體排放量已較基準
年減量 31%， 顯示公司正穩健地行走於既定的減碳路徑上 。 同時 ， 聯電亦導入內部碳定價機制，
將每噸二氧化碳當量的成本納入投資與管理決策，藉此驅動更多具實質效益的減碳行動。

圖 5. 環境目標與績效表

資料來源:UMC、國泰證期研究部整理

4. 水資源管理： 
除氣候行動外，聯電亦重視水資源管理、廢棄物減量與生物多樣性保護。公司透過提升再生水
與海淡水使用比例，降低對天然水資源的依賴，同時推動廢棄物資源化與循環經濟模式，持續
減少單位產品的環境足跡。在污染防制方面，聯電針對空氣污染與水污染設有嚴格管理機制，
確保排放表現優於法規標準，並逐步將自然相關風險與機會納入長期環境管理規劃。

5. 社會政策 
在社會政策方面，聯電以人權保障與人才發展為核心，致力打造安全、健康、多元與共融的職
場環境。公司遵循國際人權準則與責任商業聯盟行為守則，確保員工在招聘、任用、升遷與薪
酬制度中受到公平對待，並透過完善的職業安全與健康管理體系，持續降低職災風險。在人才
培育與留任方面，聯電透過教育訓練、職涯發展與員工持股等制度，強化員工向心力與長期承
諾，使人力資本成為支持公司永續成長的重要基礎。

### Page 1

向こう １ 年間の市場見通し （10月 ７ 日時点）

（注） 記載された内容は、 第一生命ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限りません。
予 想コメント
コメント
株式
日本株は、 ６ 月まではＡ Ｉ ・ 半導体関連株にけん引され、 上値を追った。 ７ 月以降、 原油価格の高止まりを受けて下落
したものの、 その間もＡ Ｉ 関連株の業績見通しは日米ともに上向いてきた。 日米欧中銀による同時利上げは逆風
となるが、 株価は業績見合いの上昇が期待される。
ドル円
Ｆ Ｒ Ｂ は ９ 月に利上げを再開した。 追加の利上げが意識されるなか、 ドル高主導の円安圧力が生じている。 もっ とも、
７ 月末頃に日米協調介入が実施されたことで、 一方的な円安進行の可能性は低下した。 当面は連続介入に対する
警戒感が残存しよう。
金利
日銀は ９ 月に政策金利を1.25％へと引き上げたうえで、 利上げペースが速まる可能性にも言及した。 インフレ圧力
が高まるなか、 円安に対する警戒もあり、 ３ ～ ４ か月に一度の利上げが標準化する可能性がある。 長期金利はイン
フレ警戒感が燻る下、 ２ ％台後半～ ３ ％台前半で推移するとみている。

### Page 1

公司 Q3 营收 277.69 亿元同⽐增 171.83% 、环⽐增 24.62% ，净利润 85.87 亿元同⽐增
173.74% ，环⽐增 8.46% ， 800G 和 1.6T ⾼端光模块持续放量。受港股上市汇兑损失约 2.54
亿元及股权投资公允价值变动损失约 4.4 亿元拖累，盈利增速低于收⼊增速。中际旭创 H 股
⾃ 980 港元的发⾏价已累计下跌约 28% ，港股基⽯投资者浮亏，淡⻢锡与⾼瓴分别浮亏超 5
亿。控股股东减持套现 31.6 亿元，⽜散章建平退出前⼗。
摘要：
中际旭创⾼速光模块业务保持强劲扩张态势，但单季盈利增速因汇兑损失及投资公允价值变动
⽽受到⼀定拖累。
29 ⽇，中际旭创公告， 2026 年前三季度营业收⼊ 695.47 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 178.13% ；归⺟净利润
222.38 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 211.81% ；扣⾮净利润 221.1 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 212.12% 。
第三季度单季营业收⼊ 277.69 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 171.83% ；归⺟净利润 85.87 亿元，同⽐增⻓

173.74% ；扣⾮净利润 90.18 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 190.13% 。以单季环⽐⼝径衡量，第三季度营业收
⼊环⽐增⻓ 24.62% ，归⺟净利润环⽐增⻓ 8.46% 。
公司表⽰，盈利增速相对收⼊滞后，主要源于港股上市带来的汇兑损失及股权投资公允价值变
动两项⾮经营性因素的拖累。公司 2026 年 7 ⽉ 30 ⽇完成⾹港联交所上市，募集资⾦约港币 609 亿
元（含超额配售权⾏使），受港元兑⼈⺠币汇率⾛弱影响，相关外币货币资⾦产⽣汇兑损失约 3.9
亿元，加之利息收⼊等因素，港股募集资⾦事项合计形成净损失，减少归⺟净利润约 2.54 亿元；
此外，第三季度确认上市公司股权投资相关公允价值变动损失，减少归⺟净利润约 4.4 亿元。
与此同时，中际旭创 H 股⾃ 980 港元的发⾏价已累计下跌约 28% ，淡⻢锡与⾼瓴各⾃浮亏逾 5 亿
元⼈⺠币，⻉莱德与摩根⼤通资管亦各浮亏约 4.6 亿元。
公司控股股东⼭东中际投资控股有限公司及其⼀致⾏动⼈则于港股上市前后合计减持逾 620 万股

A 股，套现约 31.6 亿元。

### Page 2

AI 算⼒投资热潮驱动需求，⾼端光模块持续放量
公司将业绩⾼速增⻓归因于全球 AI 算⼒基础设施建设的深⼊推进。三季报显⽰， 800G 和 1.6T 等
⾼端光模块销售持续增加，带动前三季度营业收⼊同⽐近乎翻三倍。
从单季数据看，第三季度营业收⼊ 277.69 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 171.83% ；归⺟净利润 85.87 亿元，同
⽐增⻓ 173.74% ；扣⾮净利润 90.18 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 190.13% ，扣⾮⼝径盈利增速超过收⼊端，
反映主营业务盈利质量较为稳健。
前三季度经营活动产⽣的现⾦流量净额 80.08 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 46.81% ，主要源于销售回款随收⼊
规模扩张相应增加。
港股上市与汇兑损失构成阶段性业绩扰动
财务费⽤是本季度利润增速低于收⼊增速的核⼼拖累项。前三季度财务费⽤合计约 113,600 万
元，较上年同期的约 4,344 万元⼤幅增加，主要系美元及港元对⼈⺠币汇率⾛弱导致汇兑损失约
138,700 万元，部分由约 33,800 万元利息收⼊抵消。
此外，公司前三季度确认股份⽀付费⽤，合计减少归⺟净利润约 40,500 万元。第三季度扣⾮净
利润 90.18 亿元⾼于归⺟净利润 85.87 亿元，差额主要来⾃当季约 4.4 亿元的上市公司股权投资公
允价值变动损失计⼊⾮经常性损益。
资产负债表⼤幅扩张，产能扩充投⼊加速
港股上市带来的募集资⾦显著改变了公司资产结构。截⾄三季度末，货币资⾦达 535.32 亿元，
较年初的 109.87 亿元增⻓ 387.24% ；总资产升⾄ 1,297.55 亿元，较年初增⻓ 186.50% ；归属于
上 市 公 司 股 东 的 所 有 者 权 益 达 943.63 亿 元 ， 较 年 初 增 ⻓ 217.03% ， 资 本 公 积 同 期 增 ⻓
616.22% 。
为匹配持续增⻓的在⼿订单，公司加⼤上游原材料锁定⼒度及⽣产设备投⼊。预付款项较年初
增⻓ 1068.43% ，在建⼯程增⻓ 286.46% ，存货增⻓ 88.30% ，固定资产增⻓ 75.91% 。前三季度
投资活动现⾦净流出 124.17 亿元，同⽐扩⼤逾 8.5 倍，主要⽤于购建⻓期资产及对外投资。

港股基⽯投资者普遍浮亏
中际旭创 H 股⾃上市以来持续承压，令港股上市时引⼊的 29 家基⽯投资者⼏乎悉数陷⼊账⾯亏
损。
截⾄最新收盘，中际旭创 H 股报 706.5 港元，较前⼀⽇下跌 10% ，较 980 港元的发⾏价已累计下跌
约 27.8% ，公司港股市值约 8322 亿港元； A 股报 582.05 元，较前⼀⽇下跌 8.5% ， A 股市值约
6856 亿元。
本次港股 IPO 共引⼊基⽯投资者认购总额达 34.5 亿美元（约 270 亿港元）。
按当前股价计算，淡⻢锡（旗下太⽩及 True Light 合计认购 3 亿美元）与⾼瓴旗下 HHLRA （认购
3 亿美元）各⾃浮亏约 8340 万美元，折合⼈⺠币约 5.56 亿元； JPM AM 与⻉莱德各认购 2.5 亿美
元，各浮亏约 6950 万美元（约 4.6 亿元）；云锋基⾦旗下 YF Capital 与 Aspex 各认购 1.5 亿美元，
各浮亏约 4170 万美元（约 2.78 亿元）。

中东主权财富基⾦ ADIA 、 Wellington 、 Bain Capital 、 Boyu Capital 等 13 家各认购 1 亿美元的基
⽯投资者，各浮亏约 2780 万美元（约 1.86 亿元）；阿⾥巴巴、腾讯、 CPP Investments 、
Oaktree 、 General Atlantic 等各认购 5000 万美元的投资者，则各浮亏约 1390 万美元（约 9259 万
元）。
⽬前上述持股尚未解禁，基⽯投资者暂⽆法通过⼆级市场退出。
控股股东减持套现逾 30 亿，⽜散章建平退出前⼗
尽管港股基⽯投资者浮亏待解，公司控股股东则已完成⼤规模减持。
据公司 2026 年 7 ⽉ 30 ⽇公告，控股股东中际控股及其⼀致⾏动⼈王伟修、王晓东合计减持 A 股

6,208,552 股。其中，中际控股减持 550 万股，均价 521.73 元 / 股；王晓东减持 708,552 股，均价
414.24 元 / 股；合计成交⾦额约 31.6 亿元，整体减持均价 509.46 元 / 股。
减持后，控股股东⼀⽅持股⽐例由 17.99% 降⾄ 16.53% ，减少约 1.46 个百分点。
股 权 结 构⽅ ⾯ ， 截 ⾄ 2026 年 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇ ， 前 ⼗ ⼤ 股 东 中 ， ⼭ 东 中 际 投 资 控 股 有 限 公 司 持 股
10.31% ，王伟修持股 5.92% ，⾹港中央结算有限公司持股 5.39% ， HKSCC NOMINEES
LIMITED 持股 5.32% 。
值得关注的是，知名个⼈投资者章建平已不在前⼗⼤股东之列 —— 其在截⾄ 2026 年 6 ⽉ 30 ⽇时持
股 0.53% ，位列第九⼤股东，此后退出前⼗，与港股上市导致整体股权摊薄有关。
公司限制性股票激励计划持续推进，前三季度确认股份⽀付费⽤减少归⺟净利润约 40,500 万

元。报告期内回购股票⼒度加⼤，库存股余额由年初的 4,688 万元增⾄ 49.99 亿元，增幅逾百
倍。

動態更新  印刷電路板 ‧ 台灣

台光電 
(2383.TW /2383 TT)

高階 CCL 長約需求強勁，擴產延伸至 2030 年 
重要訊息 
公司公布 3Q26 季報，毛利率及營利率 37.7%、30.7%優於共識 35.7%、
28.9%，帶動淨利達 145 億達每股盈餘 40.5 元，優共識 15%。 
評論及分析  
3Q26 漲價效應帶動獲利優共識 15%。終端組合中基礎建設營收達佔比 75-
80%，手持裝置佔比 10-15%，車用佔比 5-10%。產品組合營收中  M7 以
上佔比 65-70%，M7 與 M8 皆為 30-35%，M9 低個位數。  
4Q26 啟動新一波漲價。 4Q26 營收成長主要來自漲價( 漲幅 5-20%，但不預
期高於 2Q26)、組合升級、少量新增產能貢獻。我們預估營收 可季增 15-
20%，毛利率有望持續季增。  
高階 CCL 需求樂觀，擴產計畫延伸至 2030 年。因高階 CCL 的長約 LTA 需
求量已超越原擴產規模 ，公司決定延伸擴產計畫。 摘要如下：(1)2 026 維持
585 萬張，而  2027- 30 產能最新規劃為 900 萬張、 1045 萬張、 1195 萬
張、1525 萬張，分別年增率 54%、16%、14%、28%；觀音廠原訂 2026
年 12 月開出 30 萬張，延後至 2H27，崑山廠有機會提前於 26 年 12 月開出
10 萬張部分產能。 (2) 2027 年底載板總產能預估 120 萬張，較目前崑山廠
30 萬張增加 90 萬張。(3)M9 2027 年佔比提升至雙位數，受惠 AI Server 及
Switch 導入；M10 26 年底有機會完成認證於 2H27 量產：公司認為 M10 相
較 PTFE 具備加工良率及成本優勢。(4)高階玻纖布及 HVLP 3/4 銅箔供應相對
緊張，已提前備料與廠商 擴產協調。(5) 除 AI Server 及 Networking 外，管
理層看好機器人與低軌衛星的長期成長。  
投資建議  
在 3Q26 財報優預期下，我們上調 2027 年獲利 36%，目標價上調至 8310
元相當於 2027 年每股盈餘 32 倍本益比( 原 40 倍本益比，下調 用以反應
2H27 原物料如玻纖布漲勢趨緩、 M7 以上供應者增加、 PTFE 競爭等風險)。 
投資風險  
客戶需求不如預期；競爭對手進度超前。

圖 4: 公司概況  圖 5: 2025產品比重 
台光電子成立於 1992 年，為台灣前四大銅箔基 板(CCL)廠商之
一，全球最大無鹵素環保基板供應商，主要生產銅箔基板、黏
合片(PP)以及多層壓合板 (M/L)。台光電 2025 年終端應用營收
占比分別為行動通訊 15-20%、基礎建設 65-70%及汽車&工
業及其他 10-15%，目前單月產能可達銅箔基板 585萬張。
